Perfect! I have all the key files. Now let me compile a comprehensive analysis of the current schema and normalizer design.

## Comprehensive Analysis: Current Schema and Normalizer Design

### 1. **UCUM Mappings Structure** (`ucum_mappings.yaml`)

**Units section** (lines 11-207):
- Each unit entry has:
  - `canonical`: canonical UCUM code (e.g., "mg/dL", "kg")
  - `omop_unit_concept_id`: integer OMOP concept ID for the unit
  - `aliases`: list of alternative names/abbreviations
- Currently contains ~50 common clinical units: mass, volume, concentration, pressure, rate/renal, body metrics, dosing, time, etc.

**Value_mappings section** (lines 209-216):
- Simple flat dict: lowercased value text → OMOP value concept ID
- Currently only 6 entries (positive, negative, normal, abnormal, present, absent)
- Limited to simple yes/no or normal/abnormal qualitative values
- **Gap**: No support for ordinal scales (ECOG 0-4, numeric scores, etc.)

### 2. **Unit Normalizer** (`unit_normalizer.py`)

**Design approach**:
- Static YAML-based lookup with no LLM calls
- Loads mappings once, caches with `@lru_cache(maxsize=1)`
- Case-insensitive, alias-expandable matching

**Key functions**:
- `normalize_unit(unit_text)`: Returns `(ucum_code, omop_unit_concept_id)` or `(None, None)`
- `normalize_value(value_text)`: Returns `(normalized_text, omop_value_concept_id)` or `(None, None)`
- Both are deterministic pure functions with graceful fallback (never raises)

**Limitations**:
- `normalize_value()` only knows about the 6 hardcoded values in `value_mappings`
- No context awareness — can't differentiate "1" as ECOG score vs percentage
- No entity-specific value normalization

### 3. **Normalizer Call Site** (`structure_builder.py`, `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`)

**Context available at call site** (lines 164-213):
```python
fm = {
    "entity": str,
    "relation": str,
    "value": str,
    "unit": str | None,
    "entity_concept_id": str | None,      # From grounding
    "entity_concept_system": str | None,  # From grounding
    "omop_concept_id": str | None,        # From grounding
}
```

**Current normalization flow**:
```python
value_str = fm.get("value", "")
value_numeric, value_text = _parse_value(value_str)  # Try float, else keep as string

raw_unit = fm.get("unit")
_, unit_concept_id = normalize_unit(raw_unit)  # Lookup unit only

value_concept_id: int | None = None
if value_text and value_numeric is None:  # Only if NOT numeric
    _, value_concept_id = normalize_value(value_text)  # Lookup value
```

**Critical insight**: The normalizer receives `fm.get("entity_concept_id")` and `fm.get("omop_concept_id")` from grounding, but **currently ignores them for value normalization**. These could provide entity context!

### 4. **AtomicCriterion Model** (`shared/models.py`, lines 184-232)

**Relevant columns for value context**:
```python
entity_concept_id: str | None       # FK to grounded entity code
entity_concept_system: str | None   # System (UMLS, SNOMED, etc.)
omop_concept_id: str | None         # OMOP standard concept_id
value_numeric: float | None         # Numeric value
value_text: str | None              # Text value
unit_concept_id: int | None         # UCUM/OMOP unit concept
value_concept_id: int | None        # OMOP value/qualifier concept <-- TARGET
relation_operator: str | None       # <, >, =, has, is, etc.
```

**Observation**: The `value_concept_id` column exists and is populated (lines 216-217), but only for simple categorical mappings from the hardcoded `value_mappings` dict. This is the column we'd enhance for ordinal scales.

### 5. **Field Mapper Output** (`field_mapper.py`)

**FieldMappingItem structure** (lines 26-45):
```python
{
    "entity": str,                   # e.g., "HbA1c", "ECOG"
    "relation": str,                 # e.g., "<", ">", "has", "is"
    "value": str,                    # e.g., "7", "1", "positive"
    "unit": str | None,              # e.g., "%", None for ordinal
    "entity_concept_id": str | None,
    "entity_concept_system": str | None,
    "omop_concept_id": str | None,
}
```

**Generation context** (lines 57-162):
- Gemini receives the grounded entity's `preferred_term` and code
- Can see the full `criterion_text` for context
- Produces mappings with grounding metadata attached (lines 139-142)

### 6. **Grounding Schemas** (`grounding.py`)

**EntityGroundingResult** (lines 42-115):
```python
entity_text: str
entity_type: str
selected_code: str | None           # The grounded code
selected_system: str | None         # e.g., "UMLS", "SNOMED", "LOINC"
preferred_term: str | None
omop_concept_id: str | None         # Standard concept_id
# ... plus reasoning, candidates, etc.
```

**Key insight**: The grounding result contains `selected_system` which tells us the entity's terminology system. For LOINC-coded observations (e.g., ECOG, lab results), we could use the LOINC code to look up the proper value domain in LOINC or OMOP.

---

## Summary of Normalization Context Available

| Data Point | Available at Call Site | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Entity text | Yes (`fm["entity"]`) | Field mapper |
| Grounded entity code | Yes (`fm["entity_concept_id"]`) | Grounding |
| Grounding system | Yes (`fm["entity_concept_system"]`) | Grounding |
| OMOP concept ID | Yes (`fm["omop_concept_id"]`) | Grounding |
| Raw value text | Yes (`fm["value"]`) | Field mapper |
| Raw unit text | Yes (`fm["unit"]`) | Field mapper |
| Criterion text | **Not available** at call site | Lost after `_create_atomic_from_mapping()` |
| LOINC code for observation | **Not available** (would need separate lookup) | Could retrieve via OMOP |

---

## Key Gaps for Entity-Context-Aware Normalization

1. **LOINC observation codes**: ECOG, lab values, etc. have LOINC codes. The `omop_concept_id` field points to an OMOP concept, but we'd need to resolve that to a LOINC code or use OMOP's value domain metadata.

2. **Ordinal scale definitions**: ECOG (0-4), Karnofsky (0-100), etc. need entity-specific value concept ID mappings. These could be added to `ucum_mappings.yaml` under a new `ordinal_scales` section or in a separate `value_domains.yaml`.

3. **Qualitative value bindings**: "positive/negative" should map to SNOMED codes, but only for observational entities. For medication entities, "positive" has a different meaning.

4. **Entity system awareness**: If `entity_concept_system == "LOINC"`, we could use LOINC-specific value mappings; if "SNOMED", use SNOMED value sets.

5. **Criterion context loss**: The full criterion text is available during `build_expression_tree()` but lost by the time `_create_atomic_from_mapping()` is called. This limits disambiguation.

---

Based on the search results, here's what I found about your query:

## LOINC 89247-1 - ECOG Performance Status

LOINC Code 89247-1 is for ECOG Performance Status score. The Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status grade or score indicates a patient's level of daily functioning and physical ability.

The ECOG grade ranges from 0-5 where 0=fully active, able to carry on all pre-disease performance without restriction, 1=restricted in physically strenuous activity but ambulatory and able to carry out work of a light or sedentary nature, 2=ambulatory and capable of all selfcare but unable to carry out any work activities; up and about more than 50% of waking hours, 3=capable of only limited selfcare; confined to bed or chair more than 50% of waking hours, 4= completely disabled; cannot carry on any selfcare; totally confined to bed or chair, and 5= dead.

## OMOP CDM Integration

Records in the Standardized Vocabularies tables are derived from national or international vocabularies such as SNOMED-CT, RxNorm, and LOINC, or custom OMOP Concepts. The search results show that the MEASUREMENT table contains structured values (numerical or categorical) obtained through systematic and standardized examination or testing, including lab tests and vital signs.

For OMOP CDM measurement data, ECOG performance status would typically be recorded in the MEASUREMENT table using the LOINC concept_id for 89247-1, with the ordinal score values (0-5) stored in the **value_as_concept_id** field using appropriate OMOP concept mappings for the ordinal performance levels.

The page describes it as a quantitative measure where "0=fully active, able to carry on all pre-disease performance without restriction, 1=restricted in physically strenuous activity but ambulatory..." and continues through grade 5 (dead).

# ECOG Performance Status - Coding Systems

Based on the provided documentation, here's what I found:

## LOINC Code
The document explicitly references **LOINC 89247-1** as the standardized code for ECOG Performance Status. The profile states: "Observation resources associated with an in-scope patient with `Observation.code` LOINC `89247-1` SHALL conform to this profile."

## Value Representation
The profile specifies that ECOG performance status values are represented as **integers**, with the `value[x]` element bound to the LOINC Answer List **LL529-9** for interpretation codes.

## SNOMED CT and OMOP Concept IDs
**The document does not provide SNOMED CT concept IDs for individual ECOG grades (0-5)**, nor does it reference OMOP/standard vocabulary concept identifiers. The content focuses on the FHIR implementation structure and conformance requirements rather than detailed concept mappings across different coding systems.

## Additional Reference
The profile includes an OID identifier: **2.16.840.1.113883.4.642.40.15.42.17**

To locate SNOMED CT or OMOP codes for specific ECOG grades, you would need to consult SNOMED CT or OMOP terminology databases directly, as this implementation guide doesn't include those mappings.

## Implementation Plan: Entity-Context-Aware Value Normalization (Ordinal Scales)

### 1. Problem Statement

The current value normalization is context-free: `normalize_value("2")` returns `(None, None)` because "2" is not one of the six recognized categorical values. But for ECOG Performance Status, the value "2" has a specific clinical meaning (SNOMED 422894000 -- "Ambulatory and capable of all selfcare") and should receive both a `value_concept_id` and a `unit_concept_id` of 8527 (`{score}`).

The core challenge: value "2" is parsed as `value_numeric=2.0` by `_parse_value()`, so the existing `normalize_value()` code path is never reached (it is gated by `if value_text and value_numeric is None`). The normalization must work for **numeric** values when the entity context identifies an ordinal scale.

### 2. Architecture Overview

The design adds a new **entity-context-aware** normalization layer that sits above the existing context-free functions. The existing `normalize_unit()` and `normalize_value()` signatures remain untouched (additive constraint). A new function `normalize_ordinal_value()` is called first in `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`, and if it matches, its results override both `unit_concept_id` and `value_concept_id`.

```
                        _create_atomic_from_mapping()
                                    |
                    +---------------+------------------+
                    |                                  |
          normalize_ordinal_value()         normalize_unit() / normalize_value()
          (NEW: entity-context-aware)       (existing: context-free)
                    |
         _load_ordinal_scales()
         (reads from ucum_mappings.yaml ordinal_scales section)
```

### 3. File-by-File Changes

#### 3.1 `config/ucum_mappings.yaml`

**Full path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/config/ucum_mappings.yaml`

**Change 1: Add `{score}` to the `units` section** (after the `{ratio}` entry, around line 185):

```yaml
  # --- Ordinal score ---
  - canonical: "{score}"
    omop_unit_concept_id: 8527
    aliases: ["score", "points"]
```

This makes `normalize_unit("score")` and `normalize_unit("{score}")` work independently, even outside ordinal context.

**Change 2: Add `ordinal_scales` top-level section** (after the `value_mappings` section, at the end of the file):

```yaml
ordinal_scales:
  ecog:
    entity_aliases:
      - "ECOG"
      - "ECOG performance status"
      - "ECOG PS"
      - "Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group"
      - "Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group performance status"
      - "WHO performance status"
      - "Zubrod score"
    loinc_code: "89247-1"
    loinc_answer_list: "LL529-9"
    unit_concept_id: 8527  # {score}
    values:
      "0":
        snomed_code: "425389002"
        loinc_answer_code: "LA9622-7"
        description: "Fully active"
        omop_value_concept_id: 4175026
      "1":
        snomed_code: "422512005"
        loinc_answer_code: "LA9623-5"
        description: "Restricted in physically strenuous activity"
        omop_value_concept_id: 4174245
      "2":
        snomed_code: "422894000"
        loinc_answer_code: "LA9624-3"
        description: "Ambulatory and capable of all selfcare"
        omop_value_concept_id: 4173615
      "3":
        snomed_code: "423053003"
        loinc_answer_code: "LA9625-0"
        description: "Capable of only limited selfcare"
        omop_value_concept_id: 4174246
      "4":
        snomed_code: "423237006"
        loinc_answer_code: "LA9626-8"
        description: "Completely disabled"
        omop_value_concept_id: 4173466
      "5":
        snomed_code: "423409001"
        loinc_answer_code: "LA9627-6"
        description: "Dead"
        omop_value_concept_id: 4173467
```

**Note on `omop_value_concept_id` values:** The SNOMED codes listed (425389002, etc.) need to be verified against the actual OMOP vocabulary tables for their `concept_id` values. The IDs shown above (4175026, 4174245, etc.) are representative OMOP standard concept IDs for the corresponding SNOMED concepts. The implementer must verify these against the OMOP CDM vocabulary tables. If exact OMOP concept IDs are unavailable at implementation time, the SNOMED codes themselves are the authoritative reference and the OMOP IDs can be left as placeholder integers to be validated.

**Extensibility:** Adding Karnofsky Performance Scale would just mean adding another key under `ordinal_scales:` with the same structure:

```yaml
  karnofsky:
    entity_aliases: ["Karnofsky", "KPS", "Karnofsky performance status"]
    loinc_code: "89243-0"
    unit_concept_id: 8527
    values:
      "100": { snomed_code: "...", omop_value_concept_id: ... }
      # ...
```

#### 3.2 `tools/unit_normalizer.py`

**Full path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/unit_normalizer.py`

**Change 1: Extend `_load_ucum_mappings()` to also parse and return ordinal scale data.**

The current return type is `tuple[dict[str, tuple[str, int]], dict[str, int]]`. Adding ordinal scales means either:
- (Option A) Adding a third element to the return tuple, or
- (Option B) Creating a separate `@lru_cache` loader function for ordinal scales.

**Recommendation: Option B** -- a separate cached loader keeps the existing function's return type and cache untouched, satisfying the additive constraint. This also means existing tests don't need `cache_clear()` calls.

**New data structures and function:**

```python
# Type alias for ordinal scale lookup
# Maps lowercased entity alias -> scale key
# Then scale key -> { unit_concept_id, values: { str_value -> omop_value_concept_id } }
OrdinalScaleDef = dict[str, Any]  # full scale definition from YAML
OrdinalIndex = tuple[
    dict[str, str],           # alias_to_scale: lowered alias -> scale key
    dict[str, OrdinalScaleDef],  # scale_defs: scale key -> full definition
]


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def _load_ordinal_scales() -> OrdinalIndex:
    """Load and index ordinal scale definitions from YAML (cached).

    Returns:
        Tuple of (alias_to_scale, scale_defs) where:
        - alias_to_scale: lowercased entity alias -> scale key (e.g., "ecog")
        - scale_defs: scale key -> full ordinal scale definition dict
    """
    with open(_CONFIG_PATH) as f:
        data: dict[str, Any] = yaml.safe_load(f)

    alias_to_scale: dict[str, str] = {}
    scale_defs: dict[str, OrdinalScaleDef] = {}

    for scale_key, scale_def in data.get("ordinal_scales", {}).items():
        scale_defs[scale_key] = scale_def
        for alias in scale_def.get("entity_aliases", []):
            alias_to_scale[alias.strip().lower()] = scale_key

    return alias_to_scale, scale_defs
```

**New public function:**

```python
def normalize_ordinal_value(
    value_text: str | None,
    entity_text: str | None = None,
) -> tuple[int | None, int | None] | None:
    """Check if entity is an ordinal scale and normalize the value.

    Looks up the entity text against known ordinal scale aliases (ECOG,
    Karnofsky, etc.). If matched, maps the value to its OMOP value_concept_id
    and returns the scale's unit_concept_id ({score} = 8527).

    Args:
        value_text: Raw value string (e.g., "2", "0", "Grade 3").
            Handles both numeric strings and grade-prefixed forms.
        entity_text: Entity text from field mapping (e.g.,
            "ECOG performance status"). Used for scale identification.

    Returns:
        Tuple of (value_concept_id, unit_concept_id) if entity matches
        an ordinal scale AND the value maps to a known grade.
        Returns None if entity doesn't match any ordinal scale
        (signals caller to fall through to existing normalization).
        Returns (None, unit_concept_id) if entity matches but value
        is not a recognized grade (still sets unit to {score}).
    """
    if not entity_text or not entity_text.strip():
        return None

    alias_to_scale, scale_defs = _load_ordinal_scales()
    entity_key = entity_text.strip().lower()

    # Try exact alias match first
    scale_key = alias_to_scale.get(entity_key)

    # If no exact match, try substring/fuzzy matching
    if scale_key is None:
        for alias, skey in alias_to_scale.items():
            if alias in entity_key or entity_key in alias:
                scale_key = skey
                break

    if scale_key is None:
        return None  # Entity is not an ordinal scale

    scale_def = scale_defs[scale_key]
    unit_concept_id: int | None = scale_def.get("unit_concept_id")

    if not value_text or not value_text.strip():
        return (None, unit_concept_id)

    # Normalize value text: strip, lowercase, handle "grade X" prefix
    val = value_text.strip().lower()
    # Remove common prefixes: "grade 2" -> "2", "Grade 2" -> "2"
    for prefix in ("grade ", "grade", "stage ", "stage", "score "):
        if val.startswith(prefix):
            val = val[len(prefix):].strip()
            break

    # Handle numeric-as-float: "2.0" -> "2"
    try:
        numeric = float(val)
        if numeric == int(numeric):
            val = str(int(numeric))
    except (ValueError, TypeError):
        pass

    values_map = scale_def.get("values", {})
    value_def = values_map.get(val)
    if value_def is not None:
        value_concept_id = value_def.get("omop_value_concept_id")
        return (value_concept_id, unit_concept_id)

    # Entity matched but value is unknown grade -- still set unit
    logger.debug(
        "Ordinal scale '%s' matched for entity '%s', but value '%s' "
        "not found in scale definition",
        scale_key,
        entity_text,
        value_text,
    )
    return (None, unit_concept_id)
```

**Key design decisions in this function:**

1. **Return `None` vs `(None, None)`**: Returning `None` (not a tuple) means "entity is not an ordinal scale, fall through." Returning a tuple means "entity IS ordinal, here are the results." This is a three-valued dispatch pattern.

2. **Fuzzy matching strategy**: Uses bidirectional substring containment (`alias in entity_key or entity_key in alias`). This handles:
   - "ECOG" matching alias "ECOG performance status" (entity_key is substring of alias)
   - "ECOG performance status score" matching alias "ECOG performance status" (alias is substring of entity_key)
   - But NOT matching unrelated entities like "ECOG-related biomarker" -- wait, that would match. A safer approach would be word-boundary aware matching.

3. **Alternative fuzzy matching approach** (safer): Instead of naive substring, use a two-pass strategy:
   - Pass 1: Exact match on lowered alias.
   - Pass 2: Check if any alias is a prefix of entity_key or entity_key is a prefix of any alias.
   
   This avoids false positives from substring matches in the middle of unrelated words. However, given the clinical domain, false positives from entity names containing "ECOG" as a substring but meaning something else are extremely unlikely.

4. **"Grade" prefix stripping**: Clinical protocols may use "Grade 2" or "grade 2" instead of bare "2". The prefix stripping handles this robustly.

5. **Float-to-int normalization**: Values come through `_parse_value()` as `value_numeric=2.0`, but the YAML keys are strings like `"2"`. The function accepts the raw string (before `_parse_value`), and the call site passes `str(fm.get("value", ""))`.

Replace lines 189-194 with ordinal-first dispatch logic:

```python
    raw_unit = fm.get("unit")
    _, unit_concept_id = normalize_unit(raw_unit)

    value_concept_id: int | None = None
    entity_text = fm.get("entity")
    raw_value = str(fm.get("value", ""))

    # Try ordinal scale normalization first (entity-context-aware)
    ordinal_result = normalize_ordinal_value(raw_value, entity_text)
    if ordinal_result is not None:
        ordinal_value_cid, ordinal_unit_cid = ordinal_result
        value_concept_id = ordinal_value_cid
        if ordinal_unit_cid is not None:
            unit_concept_id = ordinal_unit_cid
    elif value_text and value_numeric is None:
        # Fallback: context-free categorical value normalization
        _, value_concept_id = normalize_value(value_text)
```

**Important behavioral change**: When an entity matches an ordinal scale:
- `unit_concept_id` is set to 8527 (`{score}`) even if `raw_unit` was `None`
- `value_concept_id` is set to the SNOMED-mapped OMOP concept for the grade
- `value_numeric` remains 2.0 (unchanged -- the value is still numeric)
- `value_text` remains `None` (unchanged -- `_parse_value("2")` returns `(2.0, None)`)

This means ordinal values are **dual-represented**: `value_numeric=2.0` for computational comparison (e.g., `ECOG <= 2`) AND `value_concept_id=<SNOMED grade 2>` for semantic meaning.

```python
from protocol_processor.tools.unit_normalizer import normalize_ordinal_value

class TestNormalizeOrdinalValue:
    """Tests for normalize_ordinal_value() — entity-context-aware ordinal scales."""

    # --- ECOG entity matching ---
    def test_ecog_exact_alias(self) -> None:
        """Exact alias 'ECOG' matches ordinal scale."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("2", "ECOG")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, unit_cid = result
        assert unit_cid == 8527  # {score}
        assert value_cid is not None  # SNOMED grade 2

    def test_ecog_full_name(self) -> None:
        """Full entity name 'ECOG performance status' matches."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("0", "ECOG performance status")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, unit_cid = result
        assert unit_cid == 8527

    def test_ecog_case_insensitive(self) -> None:
        """Entity matching is case-insensitive."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("1", "ecog performance status")
        assert result is not None

    def test_ecog_substring_match(self) -> None:
        """Fuzzy match: 'ECOG PS' should match."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("1", "ECOG PS")
        assert result is not None
        _, unit_cid = result
        assert unit_cid == 8527

    # --- ECOG value mapping ---
    def test_ecog_grade_0(self) -> None:
        """ECOG grade 0 maps to correct SNOMED concept."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("0", "ECOG")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, _ = result
        assert value_cid is not None  # Specific OMOP concept for grade 0

    def test_ecog_grade_5(self) -> None:
        """ECOG grade 5 maps to correct SNOMED concept."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("5", "ECOG")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, _ = result
        assert value_cid is not None  # Specific OMOP concept for grade 5

    def test_ecog_all_grades_distinct(self) -> None:
        """All 6 ECOG grades (0-5) map to distinct concepts."""
        concept_ids = set()
        for grade in range(6):
            result = normalize_ordinal_value(str(grade), "ECOG")
            assert result is not None
            value_cid, _ = result
            assert value_cid is not None
            concept_ids.add(value_cid)
        assert len(concept_ids) == 6  # All distinct

    # --- Value parsing edge cases ---
    def test_ecog_float_value(self) -> None:
        """'2.0' normalizes to '2' for lookup."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("2.0", "ECOG")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, _ = result
        assert value_cid is not None

    def test_ecog_grade_prefix(self) -> None:
        """'Grade 2' strips prefix and maps correctly."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("Grade 2", "ECOG")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, _ = result
        assert value_cid is not None

    def test_ecog_unknown_grade(self) -> None:
        """Unknown grade (e.g., '7') still returns unit but None value."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("7", "ECOG")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, unit_cid = result
        assert value_cid is None
        assert unit_cid == 8527

    # --- Non-ordinal entities ---
    def test_non_ordinal_entity_returns_none(self) -> None:
        """Non-ordinal entity like 'HbA1c' returns None (not a tuple)."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("7", "HbA1c")
        assert result is None

    def test_none_entity_returns_none(self) -> None:
        """None entity returns None."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("2", None)
        assert result is None

    def test_empty_entity_returns_none(self) -> None:
        """Empty entity returns None."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value("2", "")
        assert result is None

    def test_none_value_with_ordinal_entity(self) -> None:
        """None value with ordinal entity returns (None, unit_concept_id)."""
        result = normalize_ordinal_value(None, "ECOG")
        assert result is not None
        value_cid, unit_cid = result
        assert value_cid is None
        assert unit_cid == 8527

    # --- {score} unit in standard unit lookup ---
    def test_score_unit_in_normalize_unit(self) -> None:
        """'{score}' should now resolve via normalize_unit()."""
        from protocol_processor.tools.unit_normalizer import normalize_unit
        ucum, omop_id = normalize_unit("{score}")
        assert ucum == "{score}"
        assert omop_id == 8527

    def test_score_alias_in_normalize_unit(self) -> None:
        """'score' alias should resolve to {score} / 8527."""
        from protocol_processor.tools.unit_normalizer import normalize_unit
        ucum, omop_id = normalize_unit("score")
        assert ucum == "{score}"
        assert omop_id == 8527

### 4. lru_cache Invalidation Concern

The `_load_ucum_mappings()` function has `@lru_cache(maxsize=1)`. Since the YAML file is the same file, and we are adding sections to it, the cached result from the first test that loads the file will include ordinal_scales data. No issues arise because:

1. `_load_ucum_mappings()` does NOT read ordinal_scales (it reads `units` and `value_mappings`). Its cached return value is unchanged in shape.
2. `_load_ordinal_scales()` is a **separate** `@lru_cache` function that reads `ordinal_scales`.
3. Both read the same YAML file but parse different sections.

However, if any existing test modifies the YAML or uses a temp YAML, the new function also needs to handle that. Looking at the test suite, no test currently patches the YAML file -- they all use the real config. So no issue.

If in the future tests need to override the ordinal scales config, they can call `_load_ordinal_scales.cache_clear()` in a fixture.

### 5. Implementation Sequence

1. **YAML first**: Add `{score}` unit and `ordinal_scales` section to `ucum_mappings.yaml`. This is the data layer and has no code dependencies.

2. **Normalizer function**: Add `_load_ordinal_scales()` and `normalize_ordinal_value()` to `unit_normalizer.py`. This is testable in isolation.

3. **Unit tests**: Add `TestNormalizeOrdinalValue` to `test_unit_normalizer.py`. Run to verify the normalizer works correctly.

4. **Call site integration**: Update `_create_atomic_from_mapping()` in `structure_builder.py` with the ordinal-first dispatch logic and updated import.

5. **Integration tests**: Update `test_phase3_integration.py` expected values and add new ECOG test cases. Run full test suite.

6. **Verify existing tests pass**: Run all 30+ existing unit normalizer tests and all phase 3 integration tests to confirm no regressions.

### 6. Edge Cases and Risks

**Edge Case 1: Entity text is "ECOG" but the value comes through as `value_numeric` not `value_text`.** The current code calls `_parse_value("2")` which returns `(2.0, None)`. The ordinal normalizer receives `raw_value = str(fm.get("value", ""))` which is `"2"` (the original string, not the parsed float). This works correctly.

**Edge Case 2: ECOG criterion has an explicit unit (e.g., LLM extracts "score" as the unit).** The code first calls `normalize_unit("score")` which returns `("{score}", 8527)`. Then the ordinal normalizer also returns `unit_concept_id=8527`. The ordinal result overrides, but both agree. No conflict.

**Edge Case 3: Composite criteria like "ECOG 0 or 1".** This is decomposed into two field_mappings by the LLM, each with value "0" and "1". Each mapping is processed independently by `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`, so each gets its own `value_concept_id`.

**Edge Case 4: Range criteria like "ECOG <= 2".** The field mapping has `value="2"` and `relation="<="`. The ordinal normalizer maps the value "2" to grade 2's concept. This is correct -- the `value_concept_id` identifies grade 2, and the `relation_operator` of `<=` indicates "grade 2 or lower."

**Risk 1: OMOP concept ID accuracy.** The SNOMED-to-OMOP mapping for ECOG grades must be verified against the OMOP vocabulary. If wrong, the `value_concept_id` values will be incorrect. Mitigation: Add a comment in the YAML noting the source and verification date.

**Risk 2: lru_cache reads YAML once.** If the YAML file has a syntax error in the new section, the existing normalizer still works (it reads different sections). But `_load_ordinal_scales()` will fail on first call. Mitigation: The function should catch YAML parse errors gracefully and return empty indexes.
